<tr><td><b>irritate</b></td><td>/ˈɪrɪteɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒；刺激</td><td>irritate the customers<br>使顾客恼火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud advertisements before every video irritate most of our customers.  每个视频前的大声广告让我们大多数客户感到恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggravate</td><td>/ˈæɡrəveɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加重，恶化；激怒</td><td>aggravate the situation<br>使局势恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Running on the injury will only aggravate it.  带着伤跑步只会让伤势加重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annoy</td><td>/əˈnɔɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，不断烦扰</td><td>annoy the neighbors<br>不断打扰邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The barking dog began to annoy every neighbor on the quiet street.  那只不停犬吠的狗开始烦扰这条安静街上的每位邻居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exasperate</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæspəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，激怒</td><td>exasperate his parents<br>使他的父母恼火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His constant lateness exasperated even the most patient teachers.  他总是迟到，连最有耐心的老师都被激怒了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gall</td><td>/ɡɔːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（使）焦躁，激怒</td><td>galled by the remark<br>被那句话惹恼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His smug tone galled everyone sitting around the conference table.  他自鸣得意的语气激怒了围坐在会议桌旁的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inflame</td><td>/ɪnˈfleɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使加剧</td><td>inflame public anger<br>使公众愤怒加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's careless comments only inflamed public anger over the housing shortage.  市长轻率的言论只是加剧了公众对住房短缺的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nettle</td><td>/ˈnetl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>惹怒</td><td>nettled by criticism<br>被批评惹怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was clearly nettled by the reporter's rude question.  记者无礼的提问显然惹恼了她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peeve</td><td>/piːv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打扰，惹恼</td><td>peeve the audience<br>惹恼观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud phone conversations on the train really peeve the other passengers.  火车上大声打电话确实惹恼其他乘客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 provoke</td><td>/prəˈvoʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒；驱使，激起</td><td>provoke an angry response<br>引发愤怒的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His careless remark provoked an angry response from the whole audience.  他不慎的言论激起了全体观众的愤怒回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rile</td><td>/raɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，惹怒</td><td>rile up the crowd<br>激怒人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing riles him more than being interrupted in the middle of a sentence.  没有什么比话说到一半被打断更让他恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 roil</td><td>/rɔɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>roil the crowd<br>激怒人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The careless remarks of the mayor roiled the crowd outside the courthouse.  市长轻率的言论激怒了法院外的人群。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appease</td><td>/əˈpiːz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；满足</td><td>appease the angry crowd<br>安抚愤怒的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered refunds to appease its angry customers.  公司提供退款以安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacify</td><td>/ˈpæsɪfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使平静，安慰</td><td>pacify a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse sang softly to pacify the frightened child before the injection.  打针前，护士轻声唱歌安抚受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 placate</td><td>/ˈpleɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通过让步以）平息抚慰</td><td>placate the critics<br>平息批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered full refunds to placate its angriest customers.  公司提供全额退款以平息最愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 propitiate</td><td>/prəˈpɪʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抚慰，劝解</td><td>propitiate the angry gods<br>抚慰愤怒的神灵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers offered rice and flowers to propitiate the mountain spirits.  村民献上米和花来抚慰山神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soothe</td><td>/suːð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，抚慰；减轻</td><td>soothe a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soft music helped soothe the restless patient through the long night.  轻柔的音乐帮助安抚了焦躁的病人度过漫长的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>issue</b></td><td>/ˈɪʃuː/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>（有争议的）话题，议题；（使）流出</td><td>issue a statement<br>发表声明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water issued from the cracked pipe and flooded the basement overnight.  水从裂开的管道中流出，一夜之间淹了地下室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nut</td><td>/ˈnʌt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>难题；坚果</td><td>a hard nut to crack<br>难解决的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Funding the repairs is a hard nut to crack.  筹措维修资金是个难解决的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 problem</td><td>/ˈprɑːbləm/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>问题，难题</td><td>solve a problem<br>解决一个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee spent an hour defining the problem before proposing answers.  委员会花了一小时界定问题，然后才提出方案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 question</td><td>/ˈkwestʃən/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>询问，质疑；问题</td><td>question a witness<br>询问证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police questioned three men about the late-night break-in.  警方就深夜入室盗窃询问了三名男子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discharge</td><td>/dɪsˈtʃɑːrdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>释放；解雇</td><td>discharge the prisoner<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court discharged the prisoner immediately after the new evidence appeared.  新证据出现后，法庭立即释放了那名囚犯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emit</td><td>/ɪˈmɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>发出，散发，排放</td><td>emit a faint light<br>发出微弱的光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old boiler emits a low hum whenever it starts.  这台旧锅炉每次启动都会发出低沉的嗡嗡声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exude</td><td>/ɪɡˈzuːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>散发；渗出；流露</td><td>exude confidence<br>散发出自信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new captain exudes confidence whenever the game gets tight.  每当比赛紧张，这位新队长都散发出自信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 release</td><td>/riˈliːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>释放，解除束缚；发布</td><td>release the prisoners<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court ordered officials to release him from custody at once.  法庭命令官员立即将他从拘留中释放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vent</td><td>/ˈvent/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>排放，发泄；通风口</td><td>vent one's frustration<br>发泄挫败感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She vented her frustration in a long letter she never mailed.  她在一封从未寄出的长信里发泄了挫败感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 withdraw</td><td>/wɪðˈdrɔː/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>取出（款项）；撤回，撤退</td><td>withdraw money from the bank<br>从银行取钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stopped at the bank to withdraw money before the long trip.  他在长途旅行前去银行取钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jabber</b></td><td>/ˈdʒæbər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>快而不清楚地说</td><td>jabber away in Italian<br>用意大利语急促地说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tourists jabbered away in Italian throughout the entire bus ride.  游客们在整段车程中都用意大利语喋喋不休。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 babble</td><td>/ˈbæbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>发出含糊无意义的嘟囔声，胡乱说</td><td>babble on about nothing<br>没完没了地瞎扯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The toddler would babble happily for hours in his crib.  这个学步的孩子会在婴儿床里开心地咿呀乱说几个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blabber</td><td>/ˈblæbər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>唠叨，胡扯</td><td>blabber on endlessly<br>没完没了地胡扯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He blabbered about his new car until everyone stopped listening.  他不停地扯他的新车，直到没人再听。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drivel</td><td>/ˈdrɪvl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>胡言乱语</td><td>talk complete drivel<br>满口胡言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet talked complete drivel about curing serious illness with colored light.  那本小册子满口胡言，说用彩色光能治重病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gabble</td><td>/ˈɡæbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>急促而含糊地说</td><td>gabble away nervously<br>紧张地急促乱说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child gabbled excitedly about everything he had seen that day.  孩子兴奋地急促讲述那天见到的一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gibber</td><td>/ˈdʒɪbər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>语无伦次地快说</td><td>gibber with fright<br>吓得语无伦次</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was gibbering with terror and no one could understand him.  他吓得语无伦次，没人能听懂他说什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mumbo jumbo</td><td>/ˌmʌmboʊ ˈdʒʌmboʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>故弄玄虚的话，晦涩废话</td><td>legal mumbo jumbo<br>法律术语废话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract was full of mumbo jumbo that no customer could understand.  合同满是没有客户能看懂的晦涩废话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 speak slowly</td><td>/spiːk ˈsloʊli/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>缓慢地说话</td><td>speak slowly and clearly<br>缓慢清晰地说话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please speak slowly so the translator can keep up.  请说慢一点，好让译员跟上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jaded</b></td><td>/ˈdʒeɪdɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>厌倦的，没兴趣没热情的</td><td>a jaded traveler<br>厌倦旅行的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After twenty countries, even the most enthusiastic traveler becomes jaded and restless.  走过二十个国家后，就算最热情的旅行者也会厌倦而烦躁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bored</td><td>/ˈbɔːrd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>厌烦的，无聊的</td><td>bored with routine<br>厌烦例行事务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students looked bored until the experiment began to bubble.  学生们一直显得无聊，直到实验开始冒泡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tired</td><td>/ˈtaɪərd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>疲倦的；厌倦的</td><td>tired of waiting<br>厌倦了等待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 By midnight everyone was too tired to argue further.  到午夜时，所有人都累得无法再争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wearied</td><td>/ˈwɪrid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>疲惫的，厌倦的</td><td>wearied by the journey<br>被旅途累坏了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearied by months of travel, he sold the van and flew home.  被数月旅行累垮后，他卖掉面包车飞回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fed up</td><td>/fed ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>厌烦的，受够了的</td><td>fed up with excuses<br>受够了各种借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Residents are fed up with the noise from the construction site.  居民们已经受够了工地的噪音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 absorbed</td><td>/əbˈzɔːrbd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>全神贯注的，专注的</td><td>absorbed in a book<br>专注于一本书</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was so absorbed in the puzzle that he missed dinner.  他太专注于那道谜题，错过了晚饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engaged</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忙于…的；已订婚的</td><td>engaged in research<br>忙于研究工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was engaged in fieldwork throughout the entire rainy season.  整个雨季她都忙于野外调查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engrossed</td><td>/ɪnˈɡroʊst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>全神贯注的，入迷的</td><td>engrossed in conversation<br>专心交谈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children sat engrossed in the storyteller's every word.  孩子们入迷地听着讲故事人的每一个字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 interested</td><td>/ˈɪntrəstɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>感兴趣的</td><td>interested in the topic<br>对这个话题感兴趣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became interested in astronomy at the age of nine.  她九岁时开始对天文学产生兴趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intrigued</td><td>/ɪnˈtriːɡd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感到好奇的，被吸引的</td><td>intrigued by the offer<br>对这个提议感兴趣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors were intrigued by the startup's unusual pricing model.  投资者对这家初创公司奇特的定价模式很感兴趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rapt</td><td>/ræpt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>全神贯注的，入迷的</td><td>rapt attention<br>全神贯注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children listened with rapt attention as the old sailor spoke.  老水手讲述时，孩子们全神贯注地听着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jape</b></td><td>/dʒeɪp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>嘲弄</td><td>a harmless jape<br>无恶意的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He japed about his own baldness to put the guests at ease.  他拿自己的秃头开玩笑，让客人们放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jest</td><td>/dʒest/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>玩笑，戏谑</td><td>in jest<br>开玩笑地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He said it in jest, but nobody in the room was laughing.  他是开玩笑说的，但屋里没有一个人笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quip</td><td>/ˈkwɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>妙语，俏皮话；说妙语</td><td>a witty quip<br>机智的俏皮话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the hostile question with a quip that eased everyone.  他用一句俏皮话回应了敌意的提问，缓和了气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wisecrack</td><td>/ˈwaɪzˌkræk/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>俏皮话，妙语</td><td>make a wisecrack<br>说句俏皮话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He made a wisecrack about the manager's new haircut.  他就经理的新发型说了句俏皮话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banter</td><td>/ˈbæntər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>打趣，逗趣的谈话</td><td>friendly banter<br>友好的打趣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two old rivals exchanged friendly banter before the match.  两位老对手在比赛前友好地打趣了几句。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chaff</td><td>/tʃæf/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>取笑，善意地嘲弄</td><td>chaff a friend<br>取笑朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His teammates chaffed him about his terrible singing voice.  队友们拿他糟糕的歌喉取笑他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gag</td><td>/ɡæɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>笑话；玩笑之举</td><td>a running gag<br>反复出现的笑料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The same running gag appears in every episode of the series.  同一个反复出现的笑料出现在这部剧的每一集里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jive</td><td>/ˈdʒaɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>开玩笑，胡扯</td><td>stop jiving me<br>别跟我胡扯了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Quit jiving and tell me what actually happened last night.  别开玩笑了，告诉我昨晚到底发生了什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jolly</td><td>/ˈdʒɑːli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>欢乐的；开玩笑</td><td>a jolly atmosphere<br>欢快的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The jolly innkeeper kept refilling everyone's glass and telling old stories.  这位欢快的店主不停给每人续杯，还讲着旧日的故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 josh</td><td>/ˈdʒɑːʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>开玩笑，戏弄</td><td>josh a friend<br>戏弄朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They joshed him about his terrible haircut all week.  他们整周都拿他糟糕的发型开玩笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 revere</td><td>/rɪˈvɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（尤指对神的）尊崇，尊敬</td><td>revered as a hero<br>被尊崇为英雄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students in the village still revere the teacher who founded their school.  村里的学生仍然尊崇创办他们学校的那位老师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jargon</b></td><td>/ˈdʒɑːrɡən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>行业术语</td><td>technical jargon<br>专业术语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report was buried in technical jargon that ordinary readers could not follow.  这份报告埋没在普通读者无法理解的专业术语里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 argot</td><td>/ˈɑːrɡət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>行话，隐语</td><td>thieves' argot<br>盗贼的隐语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel is thick with the argot of dockworkers.  这部小说充满码头工人的行话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cant</td><td>/ˈkænt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>行话；伪善之言</td><td>political cant<br>政治套话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech was pure cant dressed up as principle.  他的演讲不过是伪装成原则的套话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dialect</td><td>/ˈdaɪəˌlekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>方言，土语</td><td>a local dialect<br>当地方言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her grandmother still speaks a dialect few young people understand.  她祖母仍说着一种少有年轻人懂的方言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jive</td><td>/ˈdʒaɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>开玩笑，胡扯</td><td>stop jiving me<br>别跟我胡扯了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Quit jiving and tell me what actually happened last night.  别开玩笑了，告诉我昨晚到底发生了什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lingo</td><td>/ˈlɪŋɡoʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>行话，隐语；外语</td><td>computer lingo<br>电脑行话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I could not follow the lawyers' lingo at all.  我完全跟不上律师们的行话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 patois</td><td>/ˈpætwɑː/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>方言，土话</td><td>the island patois<br>岛上的土话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fishermen bargained in a patois blending French and English.  渔民们用混合法语和英语的土话讨价还价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jarring</b></td><td>/ˈdʒɑːrɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺耳的</td><td>a jarring noise<br>刺耳的噪音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A jarring noise from the construction site woke the whole neighborhood early.  工地传来的刺耳噪音很早就吵醒了整个街区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grating</td><td>/ˈɡreɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺耳的，令人不适的</td><td>a grating voice<br>刺耳的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The grating noise of the saw filled the workshop.  锯子刺耳的声音充满了作坊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cacophonous</td><td>/kəˈkɑfənəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺耳的，不和谐的</td><td>a cacophonous racket<br>刺耳的喧闹声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra produced a cacophonous din while tuning up.  乐队调音时发出刺耳的嘈杂声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strident</td><td>/ˈstraɪdnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺耳的</td><td>a strident voice<br>刺耳的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her strident voice cut through the noise of the crowded hall.  她刺耳的嗓音穿透拥挤大厅里的喧闹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 melodious</td><td>/məˈloʊdiəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的，旋律优美的</td><td>a melodious voice<br>悦耳的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her melodious laugh carried across the courtyard on the still evening.  她悦耳的笑声在静夜里穿过庭院。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jaundice</b></td><td>/ˈdʒɔːndɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（因嫉妒或厌世而产生的）偏见</td><td>view with jaundice<br>带偏见看待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long unemployment had filled him with jaundice toward everyone still working.  长期失业使他对所有还有工作的人心生偏见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 animosity</td><td>/ˌænəˈmɑːsəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，憎恶</td><td>deep animosity between them<br>他们之间的深仇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of competition bred real animosity between the two families.  多年的竞争在两家之间催生了真正的敌意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 animus</td><td>/ˈænɪməs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意</td><td>personal animus against him<br>对他的个人敌意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His criticism seemed driven by personal animus rather than honest doubt.  他的批评似乎出于个人敌意，而非真诚的质疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antagonism</td><td>/ænˈtæɡənɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>敌对，对抗</td><td>open antagonism toward reform<br>对改革的公开敌视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was open antagonism between the manager and his staff.  经理与员工之间存在公开的敌对。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antipathy</td><td>/ænˈtɪpəθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶，反感</td><td>mutual antipathy<br>相互反感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There has long been an antipathy between the two departments.  这两个部门之间长期存在反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gall</td><td>/ɡɔːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（使）焦躁，激怒</td><td>galled by the remark<br>被那句话惹恼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His smug tone galled everyone sitting around the conference table.  他自鸣得意的语气激怒了围坐在会议桌旁的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hostility</td><td>/hɑːˈstɪləti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，敌对</td><td>open hostility toward<br>对…公开的敌意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan met with open hostility from local residents.  该计划遭到当地居民的公开敌视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rancor</td><td>/ˈræŋkər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，深仇</td><td>speak without rancor<br>不带怨恨地说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She described the long dispute without any rancor toward her opponents.  她讲述这场长期争端时对对手毫无怨恨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amity</td><td>/ˈæməti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>友好关系，亲善和睦</td><td>a treaty of amity<br>友好条约</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two nations signed a treaty of amity and commerce.  两国签署了友好通商条约。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jaunty</b></td><td>/ˈdʒɔːnti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻快的，活泼的</td><td>a jaunty stride<br>轻快的步伐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He walked into the office with a jaunty step and a broad smile.  他步伐轻快、笑容满面地走进了办公室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 animate</td><td>/ˈænəmeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>使有生气，激励；有活力的</td><td>animate the discussion<br>让讨论活跃起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A few well-chosen questions can animate an otherwise dull discussion.  几个精心挑选的问题就能让本来沉闷的讨论活跃起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brisk</td><td>/brɪsk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满生机的，有活力的</td><td>a brisk walk<br>快步散步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brisk walk before breakfast leaves me alert all morning.  早餐前快走一趟能让我整个上午都很清醒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 energetic</td><td>/ˌenərˈdʒetɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精力充沛的</td><td>an energetic performance<br>活力四射的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even at seventy she remains remarkably energetic and full of plans.  即使七十岁了，她依然精力充沛，计划满满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frisky</td><td>/ˈfrɪski/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼欢腾的</td><td>a frisky puppy<br>一只活泼的小狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The frisky colt raced across the field at dawn.  黎明时那匹活泼的小马驹在田野上奔跑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perky</td><td>/ˈpɜːrki/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼的，精神饱满的</td><td>a perky waitress<br>活泼的女服务员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sounded surprisingly perky for six in the morning.  早上六点，她听起来却出奇地精神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 racy</td><td>/ˈreɪsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼生动的</td><td>a racy narrative style<br>生动的叙述风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her racy narrative style keeps readers turning pages late into the night.  她生动活泼的叙述风格让读者深夜还在翻页。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spirited</td><td>/ˈspɪrɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>生气勃勃的，活跃的</td><td>a spirited debate<br>一场热烈的辩论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidates held a spirited debate about tax policy.  候选人就税收政策展开了热烈的辩论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vivacious</td><td>/vɪˈveɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼的，快活的</td><td>a vivacious personality<br>活泼的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her vivacious manner made every stranger feel welcome at the table.  她活泼的举止让每个陌生人在餐桌旁都感到受欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 staid</td><td>/ˈsteɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拘谨的，古板严肃的</td><td>a staid old bank<br>古板的老银行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The staid law firm finally allowed casual clothing.  这家古板的律所终于允许穿便装。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dead</td><td>/ˈded/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>死的；毫无生气的</td><td>a dead town at night<br>夜里死寂的小镇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The resort is completely dead in the winter months.  这个度假地在冬季几个月里毫无生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inactive</td><td>/ɪnˈæktɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不活动的，不活跃的</td><td>remain inactive for months<br>数月毫无动作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The volcano has been inactive for over two hundred years.  这座火山已有两百多年没有活动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inanimate</td><td>/ɪnˈænɪmət/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无生命的</td><td>an inanimate object<br>无生命的物体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children often speak to inanimate objects as though they could reply.  孩子常对无生命的物体说话，仿佛它们能够回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lackadaisical</td><td>/ˌlækəˈdeɪzɪkəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>懒散的，无兴趣的</td><td>a lackadaisical attitude<br>懒散的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lackadaisical attitude toward homework worried both parents.  他对作业懒散的态度让父母都很担心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 languid</td><td>/ˈlæŋɡwɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没精打采的，虚弱的</td><td>a languid summer afternoon<br>慢悠悠的夏日午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A languid summer afternoon passed with nobody willing to leave the shade.  一个慢悠悠的夏日午后过去了，没人愿意离开树荫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 languishing</td><td>/ˈlæŋɡwɪʃɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>衰弱的，无精打采的</td><td>a languishing economy<br>萎靡不振的经济</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The languishing plants revived after a week of rain.  那些萎靡的植物在一周雨后复活了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 leaden</td><td>/ˈledən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉重的，无生气的；铅灰的</td><td>a leaden sky<br>铅灰色的天空</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He walked home with leaden steps after the painful defeat.  痛苦的失败后，他步履沉重地走回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 limp</td><td>/lɪmp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、形容词</td><td>跛行，艰难缓慢地走；柔软的</td><td>limp toward the door<br>跛行走向门口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He limped slowly toward the door with his ankle wrapped in bandages.  他脚踝缠着绷带，一步一跛地慢慢走向门口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 listless</td><td>/ˈlɪstləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无精打采的，倦怠的</td><td>feel listless and tired<br>感到倦怠疲乏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fever left her listless for nearly a week.  发烧让她将近一周都无精打采。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 spiritless</del></td><td>/ˈspɪrɪtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无精打采的，没有生气的</td><td>a spiritless performance<br>毫无生气的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team gave a spiritless performance in the second half.  球队在下半场表现得毫无生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vapid</td><td>/ˈvæpɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣的，乏味的</td><td>a vapid conversation<br>乏味的谈话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party ended in vapid conversation about traffic and the weather.  聚会最后只剩下关于交通和天气的乏味闲谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jejune</b></td><td>/dʒɪˈdʒuːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣乏味的</td><td>a jejune argument<br>乏味浅薄的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics dismissed the sequel as jejune and repetitive.  评论家认为这部续集乏味且重复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arid</td><td>/ˈærɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>枯燥无趣的；干燥的</td><td>an arid lecture<br>枯燥的讲座</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor's arid lecture emptied half the seats before the break.  这位教授枯燥乏味的讲座在课间前就空了一半座位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drab</td><td>/dræb/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，无聊的</td><td>drab gray walls<br>单调的灰墙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They painted over the drab gray walls with something warmer and brighter.  他们把单调的灰墙涂上了更暖更亮的颜色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreary</td><td>/ˈdrɪri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调乏味的</td><td>a dreary afternoon<br>沉闷的下午</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a dreary afternoon indoors watching rain streak the windows.  我们在室内度过一个沉闷的下午，看着雨水划过窗玻璃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 leaden</td><td>/ˈledən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉重的，无生气的；铅灰的</td><td>a leaden sky<br>铅灰色的天空</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He walked home with leaden steps after the painful defeat.  痛苦的失败后，他步履沉重地走回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monotonous</td><td>/məˈnɑːtənəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，无变化的</td><td>a monotonous voice<br>单调的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His monotonous voice made the afternoon lecture almost impossible to sit through.  他单调的声音让下午的讲座几乎难以听完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ponderous</td><td>/ˈpɑːndərəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉重的</td><td>ponderous machinery<br>笨重的机器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ponderous machinery filled the workshop and shook the floor all day.  笨重的机器占满车间，整天震动地板。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tedious</td><td>/ˈtiːdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长乏味的</td><td>a tedious explanation<br>冗长乏味的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager gave a tedious explanation of the new filing system.  经理对新的归档系统作了冗长乏味的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weary</td><td>/ˈwɪri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>令人厌倦的；疲倦的</td><td>a weary routine<br>令人厌倦的日常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The weary meetings dragged on with no decision in sight.  这些令人厌倦的会议一拖再拖，看不到任何决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 absorbing</td><td>/əbˈzɔːrbɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，吸引人的</td><td>an absorbing novel<br>引人入胜的小说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found her account of the voyage utterly absorbing.  我觉得她对航行的叙述极其引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engaging</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>an engaging personality<br>讨人喜欢的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His engaging manner made strangers feel comfortable within minutes.  他讨人喜欢的举止让陌生人几分钟内就放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engrossing</td><td>/ɪnˈɡroʊsɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，令人专注的</td><td>an engrossing story<br>引人入胜的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary was so engrossing that nobody left early.  这部纪录片如此引人入胜，没人提早离场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gripping</td><td>/ˈɡrɪpɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>扣人心弦的，紧张的</td><td>a gripping thriller<br>扣人心弦的惊悚片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final chapter is gripping from start to finish.  最后一章从头到尾都扣人心弦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 interesting</td><td>/ˈɪntrəstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有趣的，引人入胜的</td><td>an interesting proposal<br>一个有趣的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised an interesting objection nobody had anticipated.  她提出了一个无人预料到的有趣反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intriguing</td><td>/ˌɪnˈtriːɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人好奇的，有趣的</td><td>an intriguing question<br>引人好奇的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised an intriguing point that nobody had considered.  她提出了一个无人考虑过的有趣观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 involving</td><td>/ɪnˈvɑːlvɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，扣人心弦的</td><td>an involving story<br>引人入胜的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary is more involving than any thriller this year.  这部纪录片比今年任何惊悚片都更扣人心弦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 riveting</td><td>/ˈrɪvɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的，极迷人的</td><td>a riveting account<br>引人入胜的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary offers a riveting account of life inside the mountain villages.  这部纪录片对山村生活的叙述引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 thoughtprovoking</del></td><td>/ˈθɔːt prəˌvoʊkɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人深思的</td><td>a thoughtprovoking essay<br>引人深思的文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her thoughtprovoking talk kept the audience arguing for hours.  她引人深思的演讲让观众争论了数小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jettison</b></td><td>/ˈdʒetɪsn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抛弃，放弃（想法、计划等）</td><td>jettison the proposal<br>放弃该提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company jettisoned the proposal as soon as funding became uncertain.  资金一变得不确定，公司就放弃了这项提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abandon</td><td>/əˈbændən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>放纵，尽情；放弃</td><td>with reckless abandon<br>肆意放纵地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She danced with wild abandon at the wedding reception.  她在婚宴上尽情忘我地跳舞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abdicate</td><td>/ˈæbdɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>正式放弃（权力、责任）</td><td>abdicate the throne<br>退位，逊位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king chose to abdicate in favor of his younger brother.  国王选择退位让给他的弟弟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discard</td><td>/dɪˈskɑːrd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>丢弃，抛弃</td><td>discard old habits<br>抛弃旧习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He discarded the draft and started the chapter over.  他丢掉草稿，重写了这一章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 junk</td><td>/dʒʌŋk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>废旧杂物，垃圾</td><td>a garage full of junk<br>满是废物的车库</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He finally cleared the junk out of his crowded garage.  他终于把拥挤车库里的废物清理掉了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reject</td><td>/rɪˈdʒekt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>拒绝，驳回；废品</td><td>reject the proposal<br>否决提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee voted to reject the proposal without further discussion.  委员会投票否决了该提案，不再讨论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scrap</td><td>/skræp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抛弃</td><td>scrap the whole plan<br>废弃整个计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Managers decided to scrap the whole plan and start again from scratch.  管理层决定废弃整个计划，从头再来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shed</td><td>/ˈʃed/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>摆脱，去掉；脱落</td><td>shed excess weight<br>减去多余体重</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company shed three divisions to survive the downturn.  公司剥离了三个部门以度过衰退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slough</td><td>/ˈslʌf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>摆脱，抛弃；脱皮</td><td>slough off dead skin<br>去掉死皮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The snake sloughs its skin several times a year.  这种蛇一年脱皮数次。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 throw away</td><td>/θroʊ əˈweɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>扔掉，浪费</td><td>throw away the packaging<br>扔掉包装</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not throw away the receipt; you may need it.  别扔掉收据，你可能会用到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rescue</td><td>/ˈreskju/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>解救，营救</td><td>rescue the trapped miners<br>营救被困矿工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Helicopters rescued twelve people from the flooded village.  直升机从被淹的村庄救出十二人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 salvage</td><td>/ˈsælvɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>抢救，打捞</td><td>salvage the wreckage<br>打捞残骸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Divers tried to salvage cargo from the sunken ship.  潜水员试图从沉船中打捞货物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jeopardy</b></td><td>/ˈdʒepərdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>危险</td><td>put lives in jeopardy<br>使生命处于危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cutting the safety inspections would put thousands of lives in serious jeopardy.  削减安全检查会使数千人的生命处于严重危险之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distress</td><td>/dɪsˈtres/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>痛苦，忧伤；使痛苦</td><td>cause great distress<br>造成极大痛苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden news distressed everyone in the small office.  突来的消息让小办公室里的每个人都很难受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 endangerment</td><td>/enˈdeɪndʒərmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>危害，使处于危险</td><td>child endangerment<br>危害儿童安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was charged with reckless endangerment after the crash.  车祸后他被控危害公共安全。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 imperilment</del></td><td>/ɪmˈperəlmənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>危及，陷入危险</td><td>imperilment of the crew<br>船员陷入危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report blamed the imperilment of the crew on poor training.  报告将船员陷入危险归因于训练不足。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peril</td><td>/ˈperəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>危险，风险</td><td>at your peril<br>冒着危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old bridge put every crossing traveler in real peril.  那座旧桥让每个通行的旅人都身处险境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 safeness</del></td><td>/ˈseɪfnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>安全，安全性</td><td>the safeness of the route<br>路线的安全性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parents questioned the safeness of the playground equipment.  家长质疑游乐设施的安全性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 safety</td><td>/ˈseɪfti/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>安全，安全性</td><td>safety regulations<br>安全规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers must wear helmets for their own safety.  工人必须戴头盔以保障自身安全。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 secureness</del></td><td>/sɪˈkjʊrnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>安全，牢固</td><td>the secureness of the lock<br>锁的牢固程度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers tested the secureness of every bolt on the bridge.  工程师检测了桥上每颗螺栓的牢固程度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 security</td><td>/sɪˈkjʊrəti/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>安全，保障</td><td>a sense of security<br>安全感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new fence gave the family a real sense of security.  新围栏给这家人带来真正的安全感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jest</b></td><td>/dʒest/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>玩笑，戏谑</td><td>in jest<br>开玩笑地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He said it in jest, but nobody in the room was laughing.  他是开玩笑说的，但屋里没有一个人笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 butt</td><td>/ˈbʌt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>笑柄，取笑对象</td><td>the butt of jokes<br>笑话的对象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For years he was the butt of every joke in the office.  多年来他是办公室里所有笑话的对象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 derision</td><td>/dɪˈrɪʒən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>嘲笑，嘲弄</td><td>an object of derision<br>嘲笑的对象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His proposal met with open derision from the board.  他的提议遭到董事会公开嘲笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mockery</td><td>/ˈmɑːkəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>笑柄；嘲弄，拙劣的模仿</td><td>make a mockery of justice<br>使正义成为笑柄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rushed trial made a mockery of justice in the eyes of observers.  在观察者看来，这场草率的审判使正义成了笑柄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 solemnity</td><td>/səˈlemnəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>庄严，严肃</td><td>with due solemnity<br>以应有的庄严</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coronation was conducted with all the solemnity the occasion demanded.  加冕礼以这一场合所要求的全部庄严举行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 solemn utterance</td><td>/ˈsɑːləm ˈʌtərəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>庄严的话语</td><td>a solemn utterance of vows<br>庄严的誓言宣告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ceremony closed with a solemn utterance of the oath.  仪式以庄严宣读誓词结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jibe</b></td><td>/dʒaɪb/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>意见一致</td><td>jibe with the facts<br>与事实相符</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His account of the accident does not jibe with the physical evidence.  他对这场事故的说法与实物证据不符。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accord</td><td>/əˈkɔːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>一致，符合；授予</td><td>in accord with<br>与…一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His actions were in accord with the policy he announced.  他的行动与他宣布的政策一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cohere</td><td>/koʊˈhɪr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>一致，相符；黏合</td><td>cohere with the evidence<br>与证据相符</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His account does not cohere with what the other witnesses said.  他的说法与其他证人所言并不相符。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conform</td><td>/kənˈfɔːrm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>使协调；遵照，遵从</td><td>conform to the standard<br>符合标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All new buildings must conform to strict safety codes.  所有新建筑都必须符合严格的安全规范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 correspond</td><td>/ˌkɔːrəˈspɑːnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>相符，一致；通信</td><td>correspond to the facts<br>与事实相符</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The figures in the report correspond exactly to the bank records.  报告中的数字与银行记录完全相符。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harmonize</td><td>/ˈhɑːrməˌnaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>协调一致；使和谐</td><td>harmonize with tradition<br>与传统相协调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new building harmonizes well with the older houses nearby.  这座新建筑与附近的老房子十分协调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tally</td><td>/ˈtæli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>相符，一致；计数</td><td>tally with the records<br>与记录相符</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His account of the accident does not tally with the witness statements.  他对事故的叙述与证人陈述不符。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conflict</td><td>/ˈkɑːnflɪkt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>冲突，矛盾；抵触</td><td>a conflict of interest<br>利益冲突</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge withdrew because of a possible conflict of interest.  法官因可能存在利益冲突而回避。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>jingoist</b></del></td><td>/ˈdʒɪŋɡoʊɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>极端爱国的好战分子</td><td>an outspoken jingoist<br>直言不讳的好战爱国者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist was denounced as a jingoist eager for another foreign war.  这位专栏作家被谴责为渴望再打一场对外战争的好战爱国者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chauvinist</td><td>/ˈʃoʊvənəst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>沙文主义者，盲目爱国者</td><td>a blind chauvinist<br>盲目的沙文主义者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editorial called him a chauvinist unwilling to hear other views.  社论称他是不愿听取他人意见的沙文主义者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nationalist</td><td>/ˈnæʃənələst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>民族主义者，国家主义者</td><td>a fervent nationalist<br>狂热的民族主义者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nationalist won the election by promising closed borders.  这位民族主义者以承诺封闭边境赢得选举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 superpatriot</del></td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈpeɪtriət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>极端爱国主义者</td><td>a noisy superpatriot<br>喧闹的极端爱国者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every talk show featured some superpatriot demanding war.  每个访谈节目都有极端爱国者要求开战。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 war hawk</td><td>/wɔːr ˈhɔːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>主战派，鹰派人物</td><td>a leading war hawk<br>主要的主战派人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator built his career as an unapologetic war hawk.  这位参议员以毫不掩饰的鹰派立场建立起自己的事业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dove</td><td>/ˈdʌv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>主和派，鸽派；鸽子</td><td>hawks and doves<br>鹰派与鸽派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cabinet split between hawks and doves over the treaty.  内阁就该条约分裂为鹰派与鸽派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacifist</td><td>/ˈpæsɪfɪst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>和平主义者；和平主义的</td><td>a pacifist movement<br>和平主义运动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a pacifist he refused to carry any weapon.  作为和平主义者，他拒绝携带任何武器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peacenik</td><td>/ˈpiːsnɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>反战人士，和平主义者</td><td>an aging peacenik<br>年迈的反战人士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A few peaceniks camped outside the base for months.  几名反战人士在基地外露营数月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jitters</b></td><td>/ˈdʒɪtərz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>紧张，不安</td><td>a case of the jitters<br>一阵紧张不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a bad case of the jitters before her first public performance.  首次公开演出前，她非常紧张不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 butterflies</td><td>/ˈbʌtərˌflaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>紧张不安，心里发慌</td><td>butterflies in the stomach<br>心里发慌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had butterflies in her stomach before the audition.  试镜前她心里一阵发慌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dither</td><td>/ˈdɪðər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>紧张不安；犹豫不决</td><td>in a dither<br>慌乱不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was in a dither about which offer to accept.  他为该接受哪份工作邀约而慌乱不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 Jimjams</del></td><td>/ˈdʒɪmdʒæmz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>紧张不安，心慌</td><td>get the jimjams<br>变得紧张不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Waiting for the results gave him the jimjams.  等结果让他紧张不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nerves</td><td>/ˈnɜːrvz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>紧张，神经紧张</td><td>a fit of nerves<br>一阵紧张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nerves got the better of her during the opening speech.  开场演讲时紧张情绪占了上风。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shakes</td><td>/ˈʃeɪks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>颤抖，紧张发抖</td><td>have the shakes<br>浑身发抖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 By the third cup of coffee he had the shakes.  喝到第三杯咖啡时他浑身发抖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shivers</td><td>/ˈʃɪvərz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>颤抖，哆嗦</td><td>send shivers down the spine<br>令人脊背发凉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The howling wind sent shivers down my spine.  呼啸的风让我脊背发凉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 willies</td><td>/ˈwɪliz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>紧张，心里发毛</td><td>give someone the willies<br>让某人心里发毛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That empty hallway always gives me the willies.  那条空走廊总让我心里发毛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aplomb</td><td>/əˈplɑːm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>沉着，泰然自若</td><td>with great aplomb<br>非常从容地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She handled the hostile interview with remarkable aplomb.  她极为从容地应对了那场充满敌意的采访。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm</td><td>/ˈkɑːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词、名词</td><td>平静的；使镇静</td><td>calm down quickly<br>很快平静下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a calm voice until the children settled.  她用平静的声音说话，直到孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 composure</td><td>/kəmˈpoʊʒər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>镇静，沉着</td><td>regain one's composure<br>重新恢复镇静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lost his composure only once, when the verdict was read.  只有在宣读判决时，他才失了一次镇静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 equanimity</td><td>/ˌiːkwəˈnɪmɪti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平静，镇定</td><td>with equanimity<br>以平静的心态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She accepted the bad news with surprising equanimity.  她以出人意料的平静接受了这个坏消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 imperturbability</del></td><td>/ˌɪmpərˌtɜːrbəˈbɪləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冷静，泰然自若</td><td>admirable imperturbability<br>令人钦佩的镇定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She handled the interrogation with complete imperturbability.  她以完全的镇定应对了审问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 self-possession</td><td>/ˌself pəˈzeʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>沉着，泰然自若</td><td>remarkable self-possession<br>非凡的沉着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist showed unusual self-possession on stage.  这位年轻钢琴家在台上表现出罕见的沉着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tranquility</td><td>/træŋˈkwɪləti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>宁静，淡定</td><td>an air of tranquility<br>宁静的气息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He enjoyed the tranquility of the snowy fields at dusk.  他享受黄昏时白雪覆盖的田野的静谧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jocular</b></td><td>/ˈdʒɑːkjələr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>搞笑的，欢乐的</td><td>a jocular remark<br>诙谐的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His jocular tone kept the tense meeting from turning truly hostile.  他诙谐的语气让紧张的会议没有真正翻脸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 blithesome</del></td><td>/ˈblaɪðsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢乐的，无忧无虑的</td><td>a blithesome tune<br>欢快的曲调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A blithesome tune drifted out of the open window.  一支欢快的曲调从敞开的窗户飘出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 festive</td><td>/ˈfestɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢乐的，节日的</td><td>a festive mood<br>欢乐的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole street took on a festive air by evening.  到傍晚整条街都染上了节日气息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gleeful</td><td>/ˈɡliːfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢欣的，兴高采烈的</td><td>a gleeful grin<br>兴高采烈的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children were gleeful when the snow closed their school.  大雪停课时孩子们兴高采烈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 jocund</del></td><td>/ˈdʒɑːkənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢快的，高兴的</td><td>a jocund gathering<br>欢快的聚会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The jocund crowd sang and danced until the last lantern went out.  欢快的人群唱歌跳舞，直到最后一盏灯笼熄灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jovial</td><td>/ˈdʒoʊviəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快的</td><td>a jovial host<br>愉快的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our jovial host kept the guests laughing throughout the long winter evening.  我们愉快的主人让客人们在漫长的冬夜里笑声不断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mirthful</td><td>/ˈmɜːrθfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢乐的，充满欢笑的</td><td>a mirthful crowd<br>欢笑的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His mirthful laugh carried across the courtyard and made everyone smile.  他欢快的笑声传遍庭院，让所有人都笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lachrymose</td><td>/ˈlækrɪmoʊs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>催人泪下的，悲伤的</td><td>a lachrymose farewell<br>催人泪下的告别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's lachrymose ending left half the theater reaching for tissues.  这部电影催人泪下的结尾让半个影院的人去找纸巾。</td></tr>
</tbody>

<tr><td>↳反 saturnine</td><td>/ˈsætərnaɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的，阴沉的</td><td>a saturnine temperament<br>阴沉的性情</td></tr>

<tr><td><b>jog</b></td><td>/dʒɑːɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>轻推；唤起（记忆）</td><td>jog one's memory<br>唤起某人的记忆</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incite</td><td>/ɪnˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>incite a riot<br>煽动暴乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was charged with inciting a riot outside the county courthouse.  他被指控在县法院外煽动暴乱。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 instigate</td><td>/ˈɪnstɪɡeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>instigate a rebellion<br>煽动叛乱</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pique</td><td>/piːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起（兴趣）；使生气</td><td>pique her curiosity<br>激起她的好奇心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange title piqued her curiosity, so she borrowed the book.  奇怪的书名激起了她的好奇心，于是她借走了那本书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remind</td><td>/riˈmaɪnd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提醒，使想起</td><td>remind me later<br>稍后提醒我</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please remind me to call the dentist tomorrow.  请提醒我明天给牙医打电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stimulate</td><td>/ˈstɪmjəˌleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，激发；促进</td><td>stimulate economic growth<br>促进经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm rain stimulated the seeds to sprout within a week.  温暖的雨水刺激种子一周内发芽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stir</td><td>/ˈstɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，煽动；搅拌</td><td>stir up anger<br>激起怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The article stirred up anger among local residents.  这篇文章激起了当地居民的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 allay</td><td>/əˈleɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，平息</td><td>allay public fears<br>平息公众恐慌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The statement was meant to allay concerns about safety.  这份声明意在减轻人们对安全的担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 alleviate</td><td>/əˈliːvieɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>缓和，减轻</td><td>alleviate the pain<br>减轻疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A warm bath helped alleviate the soreness in his back.  热水澡帮他缓解了背部的酸痛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ease</td><td>/ˈiːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>减轻，缓和；容易</td><td>ease the pain<br>减轻疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The medicine eased her headache within minutes of the first dose.  这药在服下第一剂几分钟内就缓解了她的头痛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mitigate</td><td>/ˈmɪtɪɡeɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，使缓和</td><td>mitigate the damage<br>减轻损害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Planting trees can mitigate the effects of summer heat.  种树可以减轻夏季高温的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mollify</td><td>/ˈmɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>平息，抚慰，缓和</td><td>mollify an angry customer<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sincere apology from the manager mollified the furious client.  经理诚挚的道歉安抚了这位愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 palliate</del></td><td>/ˈpælieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，缓和；掩饰</td><td>palliate the symptoms<br>缓解症状</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new drug can only palliate the pain, not cure the disease.  这种新药只能减轻疼痛，无法治愈疾病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 relieve</td><td>/rɪˈliːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，缓解；换班替换</td><td>relieve the pressure<br>缓解压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A short walk relieved the tension in her shoulders.  短暂散步缓解了她肩膀的紧张。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soothe</td><td>/suːð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，抚慰；减轻</td><td>soothe a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soft music helped soothe the restless patient through the long night.  轻柔的音乐帮助安抚了焦躁的病人度过漫长的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jolt</b></td><td>/dʒoʊlt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>惊吓</td><td>jolt the nation<br>震惊全国</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 News of the factory closing jolted the entire town into sudden panic.  工厂关闭的消息震惊了整个镇子，使其陷入突然的恐慌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appall</td><td>/əˈpɔːl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使惊恐</td><td>appall the audience<br>使观众震惊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The violence in the film seemed to appall even veteran critics.  影片中的暴力似乎连资深影评人都感到惊恐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 floor</td><td>/ˈflɔːr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使震惊，使不知所措</td><td>floor someone with news<br>用消息震住某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unexpected verdict floored everyone sitting in the crowded courtroom.  意外的判决震住了坐在拥挤法庭里的所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shake up</td><td>/ʃeɪk ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使震惊；彻底改组</td><td>shake up the industry<br>震动整个行业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The merger will shake up the entire publishing industry.  这次合并将震动整个出版业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jot</b></td><td>/dʒɑːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>草草记下；少量</td><td>jot down a number<br>记下一个号码</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Let me jot down your number before I forget it completely.  让我在彻底忘掉之前把你的号码草草记下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 log</td><td>/ˈlɔːɡ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>记录，登记</td><td>log the results<br>记录结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nurses log every dose in the patient's chart.  护士把每次用药都记录在病历上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mark</td><td>/ˈmɑːrk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>记下，标记</td><td>mark the date<br>记下日期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She marked the passage with a thin pencil line.  她用细铅笔线标记了那段文字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 put down</td><td>/pʊt ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>记下，写下</td><td>put down a few notes<br>记下几条笔记</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He put down the address before he forgot it.  他趁没忘先记下了地址。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 register</td><td>/ˈredʒɪstər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>登记，记录</td><td>register a complaint<br>登记一项投诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You must register your bicycle with the city office.  你必须在市政办公室登记自行车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 report</td><td>/riˈpɔːrt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>报告，记录</td><td>report the findings<br>报告调查结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team reported its findings in a twelve-page memo.  团队用十二页备忘录报告了调查结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 set down</td><td>/set ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>写下，记下</td><td>set down the rules<br>写下规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The founders set down the rules in a single page.  创始人把规则写在了一页纸上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 take down</td><td>/teɪk ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>贬低，使丢脸；记下</td><td>take someone down a peg<br>使某人收敛傲气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reporter tried to take him down in front of the whole crowd.  记者试图在众人面前贬低他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 write down</td><td>/raɪt ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>减记账面价值；写下</td><td>write down the asset value<br>减记资产价值</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bank had to write down billions in bad loans.  银行不得不减记数十亿的不良贷款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jovial</b></td><td>/ˈdʒoʊviəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快的</td><td>a jovial host<br>愉快的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our jovial host kept the guests laughing throughout the long winter evening.  我们愉快的主人让客人们在漫长的冬夜里笑声不断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 blithesome</del></td><td>/ˈblaɪðsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢乐的，无忧无虑的</td><td>a blithesome tune<br>欢快的曲调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A blithesome tune drifted out of the open window.  一支欢快的曲调从敞开的窗户飘出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 festive</td><td>/ˈfestɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢乐的，节日的</td><td>a festive mood<br>欢乐的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole street took on a festive air by evening.  到傍晚整条街都染上了节日气息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gay</td><td>/ˈɡeɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>快乐的，欢快的</td><td>gay laughter<br>欢快的笑声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Gay music and bright lanterns filled the village square that evening.  那晚欢快的音乐和明亮的灯笼充满了村中广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gleeful</td><td>/ˈɡliːfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢欣的，兴高采烈的</td><td>a gleeful grin<br>兴高采烈的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children were gleeful when the snow closed their school.  大雪停课时孩子们兴高采烈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jocular</td><td>/ˈdʒɑːkjələr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>搞笑的，欢乐的</td><td>a jocular remark<br>诙谐的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His jocular tone kept the tense meeting from turning truly hostile.  他诙谐的语气让紧张的会议没有真正翻脸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 jocund</del></td><td>/ˈdʒɑːkənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢快的，高兴的</td><td>a jocund gathering<br>欢快的聚会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The jocund crowd sang and danced until the last lantern went out.  欢快的人群唱歌跳舞，直到最后一盏灯笼熄灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jolly</td><td>/ˈdʒɑːli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>欢乐的；开玩笑</td><td>a jolly atmosphere<br>欢快的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The jolly innkeeper kept refilling everyone's glass and telling old stories.  这位欢快的店主不停给每人续杯，还讲着旧日的故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mirthful</td><td>/ˈmɜːrθfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢乐的，充满欢笑的</td><td>a mirthful crowd<br>欢笑的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His mirthful laugh carried across the courtyard and made everyone smile.  他欢快的笑声传遍庭院，让所有人都笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lachrymose</td><td>/ˈlækrɪmoʊs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>催人泪下的，悲伤的</td><td>a lachrymose farewell<br>催人泪下的告别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's lachrymose ending left half the theater reaching for tissues.  这部电影催人泪下的结尾让半个影院的人去找纸巾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 saturnine</td><td>/ˈsætərnaɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的，阴沉的</td><td>a saturnine temperament<br>阴沉的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His saturnine expression discouraged anyone from starting a cheerful conversation.  他阴沉的表情让人不敢开启轻松的话题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>jubilant</b></td><td>/ˈdʒuːbɪlənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喜悦的</td><td>a jubilant crowd<br>欢欣的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nominee gave a short speech before the jubilant crowd.  被提名人在欢欣鼓舞的人群前发表了简短讲话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exulting</td><td>/ɪɡˈzʌltɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狂喜的，欢欣鼓舞的</td><td>an exulting shout<br>欢呼雀跃的叫喊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Exulting fans poured onto the field after the whistle.  哨声后狂喜的球迷涌入场地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glorying</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːriɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢欣的，得意扬扬的</td><td>glorying in victory<br>为胜利而欢欣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The glorying crew waved flags from the deck.  欢欣的船员在甲板上挥舞旗帜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rejoicing</td><td>/rɪˈdʒɔɪsɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>欢欣的，喜悦的</td><td>a rejoicing crowd<br>欢欣的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A rejoicing crowd filled the square until dawn.  欢欣的人群直到黎明还挤满广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 triumphant</td><td>/traɪˈʌmfənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>胜利的，得意洋洋的</td><td>a triumphant return<br>凯旋而归</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a triumphant return to the stage after surgery.  手术后她胜利地重返舞台。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lachrymose</td><td>/ˈlækrɪmoʊs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>催人泪下的，悲伤的</td><td>a lachrymose farewell<br>催人泪下的告别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's lachrymose ending left half the theater reaching for tissues.  这部电影催人泪下的结尾让半个影院的人去找纸巾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 saturnine</td><td>/ˈsætərnaɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的，阴沉的</td><td>a saturnine temperament<br>阴沉的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His saturnine expression discouraged anyone from starting a cheerful conversation.  他阴沉的表情让人不敢开启轻松的话题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>judicious</b></td><td>/dʒuˈdɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，慎重的</td><td>a judicious choice<br>明智的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A judicious use of savings carried the family through two difficult years.  明智地使用积蓄让这家人度过了两个艰难的年头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intelligent</td><td>/ɪnˈtelədʒənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，聪明的</td><td>an intelligent decision<br>明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Selling the old plant was an intelligent decision.  卖掉旧厂是个明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 judgmatic</del></td><td>/dʒʌdʒˈmætɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有判断力的，明断的</td><td>a judgmatic reply<br>有判断力的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His judgmatic reply calmed both sides of the dispute.  他有判断力的回答平息了争端双方。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prudent</td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tactical</td><td>/ˈtæktɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>策略上明智的；战术的</td><td>a tactical retreat<br>策略性的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Staying silent was a tactical choice, not weakness.  保持沉默是策略上的选择，而非软弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wise</td><td>/ˈwaɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，有智慧的</td><td>a wise decision<br>明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was wise of her to save money before quitting the job.  她辞职前先存钱，很明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 daft</td><td>/dæft/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a daft suggestion<br>愚蠢的建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Selling the only truck in winter was a truly daft decision.  在冬天卖掉唯一的卡车实在是个愚蠢的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imprudent</td><td>/ɪmˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的</td><td>an imprudent investment<br>不明智的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Borrowing so much money at that rate was clearly imprudent.  以那样的利率借这么多钱显然是不明智的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inadvisable</td><td>/ˌɪnədˈvaɪzəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，不可取的</td><td>inadvisable at this stage<br>现阶段不可取</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors said surgery was inadvisable for a patient so weak.  医生说对如此虚弱的病人手术并不可取。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inexpedient</del></td><td>/ˌɪnɪkˈspiːdiənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，不合时宜的</td><td>an inexpedient remark<br>不合时宜的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Raising the issue now would be inexpedient and might delay the vote.  现在提出这个问题并不明智，还可能拖延表决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indiscreet</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈskriːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不慎重的，轻率的</td><td>an indiscreet comment<br>轻率的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One indiscreet email to a colleague cost him the promotion.  一封写给同事的轻率邮件让他失去了升职机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impolitic</td><td>/ɪmˈpɑːlətɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，不策略的</td><td>an impolitic question<br>不策略的提问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was impolitic to mention his failure at the party.  在聚会上提他的失败很不明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unwise</td><td>/ʌnˈwaɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，愚蠢的</td><td>an unwise decision<br>不明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Borrowing more money now would be deeply unwise.  现在再借钱是很不明智的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>juggernaut</b></td><td>/ˈdʒʌɡərnɔːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>无法阻挡的力量，摧毁一切的强大力量</td><td>an unstoppable juggernaut<br>无法阻挡的强大力量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Industrialization became an unstoppable juggernaut that reshaped every corner of society.  工业化成了一股不可阻挡的巨大力量，重塑了社会的每个角落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 steamroller</td><td>/ˈstiːmˌroʊlər/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>压倒性力量；强行推进</td><td>steamroller the opposition<br>压倒反对声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The majority steamrollered the bill through in one night.  多数派一夜之间强行通过了议案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ken</b></td><td>/ken/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>视野范围</td><td>beyond one's ken<br>超出某人的理解范围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Advanced quantum mechanics lies well beyond the ken of most undergraduates.  高等量子力学远远超出大多数本科生的理解范围。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sight</td><td>/ˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>视野，视力；景象</td><td>out of sight<br>看不见，在视野外</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ship stayed in sight until fog closed in.  那艘船一直在视野内，直到大雾合围。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>kidnap</b></td><td>/ˈkɪdnæp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>绑架勒索</td><td>kidnap for ransom<br>绑架勒索赎金</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Armed men tried to kidnap the businessman's son for a huge ransom.  武装人员企图绑架这位商人的儿子以索取巨额赎金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abduct</td><td>/æbˈdʌkt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>绑架，诱拐</td><td>abduct a witness<br>绑架一名证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two men tried to abduct the businessman outside his office.  两名男子试图在办公室外绑架这位商人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 release</td><td>/riˈliːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>释放，解除束缚；发布</td><td>release the prisoners<br>释放囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court ordered officials to release him from custody at once.  法庭命令官员立即将他从拘留中释放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 set free</td><td>/set ˈfriː/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>释放，放走</td><td>set the hostages free<br>释放人质</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court ordered the prisoner set free after new evidence.  新证据出现后法院下令释放该囚犯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>kindle</b></td><td>/ˈkɪndl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>点燃</td><td>kindle a fire<br>点燃火堆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single spark can kindle a dangerous forest fire.  一颗火星就能点燃危险的森林大火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 enkindle</del></td><td>/ɪnˈkɪndl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>点燃，激起</td><td>enkindle enthusiasm<br>激起热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One good teacher can enkindle a lifelong curiosity.  一位好老师能点燃终身的好奇心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ignite</td><td>/ɪɡˈnaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>点燃，使燃烧；激起（感情）</td><td>ignite public outrage<br>激起公众愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The verdict ignited public outrage across the entire country within a few hours.  判决在几小时内就激起了全国的公众愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inflame</td><td>/ɪnˈfleɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使加剧</td><td>inflame public anger<br>使公众愤怒加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's careless comments only inflamed public anger over the housing shortage.  市长轻率的言论只是加剧了公众对住房短缺的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 torch</td><td>/ˈtɔːrtʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>纵火烧毁；火炬</td><td>torch a building<br>烧毁一栋建筑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vandals torched two abandoned cars in the lot behind the warehouse.  破坏者烧毁了仓库后停车场里两辆废弃的汽车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 douse</td><td>/daʊs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扑灭（火或灯）；浇透</td><td>douse the flames<br>扑灭火焰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters worked all night to douse the flames on the upper floors.  消防员整夜奋战，扑灭了高层的火焰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extinguish</td><td>/ɪkˈstɪŋɡwɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>熄灭</td><td>extinguish the fire<br>扑灭大火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters worked all night to extinguish the burning warehouse.  消防员整夜工作扑灭燃烧的仓库。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quench</td><td>/kwentʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解（渴）；熄灭</td><td>quench one's thirst<br>解渴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A glass of cold lemonade quickly quenched my desperate thirst.  一杯冰镇柠檬水很快解了我的焦渴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 put out</td><td>/ˈpʊt ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使不便，麻烦；扑灭</td><td>put you out<br>麻烦到你</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I hope my early arrival did not put you out too much.  希望我来得早没太麻烦你。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 snuff out</td><td>/snʌf ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>扑灭；终止，毁灭</td><td>snuff out a candle<br>掐灭蜡烛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single storm can snuff out years of careful planting.  一场风暴就能毁掉多年的精心种植。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>kindred</b></td><td>/ˈkɪndrəd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>同类的，相似的</td><td>a kindred spirit<br>志趣相投的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We recognized each other as kindred spirits within the first five minutes.  头五分钟我们就认出彼此是志趣相投的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agreeable</td><td>/əˈɡriːəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉悦的；相一致的</td><td>an agreeable companion<br>一个讨人喜欢的同伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found him an agreeable companion on the long train journey.  她觉得他是长途火车旅程中一位讨人喜欢的同伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amicable</td><td>/ˈæmɪkəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>友善的，和睦的</td><td>an amicable settlement<br>友好的和解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dispute ended in an amicable agreement between neighbors.  这场纠纷以邻里间的友好协议告终。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compatible</td><td>/kəmˈpætəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>一致的，能共存的</td><td>compatible with the system<br>与该系统兼容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new driver is compatible with both older printers.  新驱动与两款旧打印机都兼容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 congenial</td><td>/kənˈdʒiːniəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>和善的，友好的</td><td>a congenial host<br>一位和善的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our congenial host made every guest feel completely at home.  我们和善的主人让每位客人都感到自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frictionless</td><td>/ˈfrɪkʃənləs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无摩擦的，顺畅融洽的</td><td>a frictionless partnership<br>融洽顺畅的合作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two departments enjoy a frictionless working relationship.  两个部门保持着顺畅融洽的工作关系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unanimous</td><td>/juˈnænɪməs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>一致同意的</td><td>a unanimous decision<br>一致通过的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The jury reached a unanimous verdict after only two hours of discussion.  陪审团仅讨论两小时便达成了一致的裁决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 united</td><td>/juˈnaɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>联合的，团结一致的</td><td>a united front<br>统一战线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villages stayed united against the plan to divert the river.  各村庄团结一致反对改道河流的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disagreeable</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈɡriːəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人不快的；脾气坏的</td><td>a disagreeable task<br>令人不快的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cleaning the drains was a thoroughly disagreeable job.  清理下水道是件极其讨人厌的活儿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discordant</td><td>/dɪˈskɔːrdənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不一致的，不和谐的</td><td>discordant opinions<br>不一致的意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Discordant voices from the back ruined the quiet ceremony.  后面不和谐的声音破坏了安静的仪式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 disharmonious</del></td><td>/ˌdɪshɑrˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不和谐的，不一致的</td><td>a disharmonious relationship<br>不和睦的关系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their disharmonious partnership collapsed within a single season.  他们不和的合作关系在一个赛季内就崩溃了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disunited</td><td>/ˌdɪsjuˈnaɪtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>分裂的，不团结的</td><td>a disunited party<br>分裂的政党</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A disunited opposition could not challenge the ruling coalition.  分裂的反对派无法挑战执政联盟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incompatible</td><td>/ˌɪnkəmˈpætəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相容的，矛盾的</td><td>incompatible goals<br>互不相容的目标</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rapid growth and strict conservation seemed incompatible goals here.  在这里快速发展与严格保护似乎是矛盾的目标。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inharmonious</del></td><td>/ˌɪnhɑːrˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不和谐的，不协调的</td><td>inharmonious colors<br>不协调的颜色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new wing looked inharmonious beside the old stone chapel.  新翼楼在古老石砌小教堂旁显得不协调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 uncongenial</del></td><td>/ˌʌnkənˈdʒiːniəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不投缘的，令人不快的</td><td>an uncongenial colleague<br>不投缘的同事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He found the damp coastal climate thoroughly uncongenial and moved inland.  他觉得潮湿的海滨气候极不宜人，于是搬到了内陆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>knack</b></td><td>/næk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>诀窍，聪明的做法</td><td>a knack for languages<br>学语言的天分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My sister has a remarkable knack for learning foreign languages quickly.  我妹妹在快速学外语上有惊人的天分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 artifice</td><td>/ˈɑːrtəfɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>诡计，欺骗；巧妙手段</td><td>use clever artifice<br>使用巧妙的诡计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her apparent shyness was pure artifice designed to disarm her rivals.  她表面的羞怯纯属伪装，为的是麻痹对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 device</td><td>/dɪˈvaɪs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>手段，策略；装置</td><td>a clever narrative device<br>巧妙的叙事手法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Flashbacks are a device she uses in every novel.  倒叙是她每部小说都用的手法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gambit</td><td>/ˈɡæmbɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>开局策略，手段</td><td>an opening gambit<br>开场的策略</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His opening gambit was to praise his rival's work.  他的开场策略是赞扬对手的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ploy</td><td>/ˈplɔɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>计策，手段</td><td>a clever marketing ploy<br>巧妙的营销手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The free samples were simply a clever marketing ploy.  免费样品只是一个巧妙的营销手段。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scheme</td><td>/ˈskiːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>阴谋，计划</td><td>a scheme to defraud investors<br>诈骗投资者的阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They devised a scheme to avoid paying any taxes.  他们设计了一个逃税的计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sleight</td><td>/ˈslaɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>手法，巧计；灵巧</td><td>sleight of hand<br>手法巧妙的骗术</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The accountant hid the losses by sheer sleight of hand.  这位会计仅靠巧妙手法掩盖了亏损。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stratagem</td><td>/ˈstrætədʒəm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>谋略，策略</td><td>a military stratagem<br>一项军事谋略</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general won the pass by a simple stratagem.  将军用一个简单的谋略夺取了关隘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foolishness</td><td>/ˈfuːlɪʃnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>愚蠢，蠢行</td><td>sheer foolishness<br>十足的愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lending him the car again was sheer foolishness.  再把车借给他实在是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>kilter</b></td><td>/ˈkɪltər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>平衡</td><td>out of kilter<br>失去平衡，不正常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old washing machine has been out of kilter since the move.  搬家之后这台旧洗衣机就一直不正常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 form</td><td>/ˈfɔːrm/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>构成，形成；形式</td><td>form a committee<br>组成一个委员会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Six neighbors formed a committee to repair the shared path.  六位邻居组成委员会修理共用的小路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 keeping</td><td>/ˈkiːpɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>协调，一致；保管</td><td>in keeping with tradition<br>与传统相符</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The extension is in keeping with the old facade.  这处扩建与旧立面协调一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 order</td><td>/ˈɔːrdər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>秩序，条理；命令</td><td>restore public order<br>恢复公共秩序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police worked all night to restore order in the city.  警察通宵工作以恢复城市的秩序。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shape</td><td>/ˈʃeɪp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使成形，塑造</td><td>shape public opinion<br>塑造公众舆论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early experiences shape the way children see the world.  早期经历塑造了孩子看世界的方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>knit</b></td><td>/nɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>连接，联系</td><td>knit two communities together<br>把两个社区联结起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new festival helped knit two divided communities closely together again.  这个新节庆帮助把两个分裂的社区重新紧密联结起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bind</td><td>/ˈbaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>捆绑，束缚；约束</td><td>bind his hands<br>捆住他的手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty binds both countries to reduce their emissions.  该条约约束两国削减排放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bond</td><td>/ˈbɑːnd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使结合，联结</td><td>bond the layers together<br>把各层黏合起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The glue bonds metal to glass in seconds.  这种胶数秒内就能把金属与玻璃黏合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 combine</td><td>/kəmˈbaɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>结合，联合</td><td>combine two elements<br>结合两种元素</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The recipe combines sweet and sour flavors very successfully.  这道菜谱把甜味和酸味结合得很成功。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 connect</td><td>/kəˈnekt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>连接，联结</td><td>connect the two wires<br>连接两根导线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A narrow tunnel connects the two halves of the building.  一条窄隧道把大楼的两半连起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fasten</td><td>/ˈfæsən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>系紧，固定，连接</td><td>fasten the straps<br>系紧背带</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please fasten your seatbelt before the plane begins to taxi.  飞机开始滑行前请系好安全带。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
